Fund Descriptions
1. General Fund (1xx) – Accounts for revenue and expenses associated with the general operations of the 
District.
2. Debt Retirement Fund (3xx) – Accounts for revenue and expenses associated with the District’s debt 
obligations.
3. Capital Projects Fund (414, 415) – Accounts for the bond proceeds and expenses associated with the District’s 
bond projects (not included in this presentation). 
4. Capital Maintenance Fund (499) – Accounts for the expenses associated with the District’s capital 
maintenance projects.
5. Food Service Fund (520) – Accounts for revenue and expenses associated with the District’s food service 
program.
6. TCC Grant Fund (529) – Accounts for the revenue and expenses associated with the District’s operations of 
the Troy Career Center
7. Community Service Fund (530) – Accounts for revenue and expenses associated with the District’s fee for 
service programs including PAL, CARE and TOTS.
8. Facility Rental Fund (535) – Accounts for the revenue and expenses associated with the District’s rental of 
various facilities.

• Benefit Increases

3.   RECOGNITION  
A.   TSD Mission Moment—Ed Morykwas 
Tonight we honor someone who has become an integral part of the Troy 
School District, going above and beyond the call of duty to help showcase our 
students and staff and tell our stories. Ed Morykwas is retiring as the 
photographer/photojournalist/webmaster of School Life Troy, our very popular 
monthly newspaper. Ed helped to launch School Life two years ago and in that 
time, became a regular fixture in every school, at every event and in every 
celebration. With his trademark enthusiasm, talent and passion for the work 
that goes on in our classrooms, Ed has helped shine a spotlight on the 
achievements and efforts of our students and staff. His photos tell our stories, 
but his personality and never -ending dedication builds confidence for our 
students and shapes the way we see ourselves. 
Ed, we thank you for your exemplary service and commitment to the Troy 
School District and we wish you well in your retirement. Next year, there will 
be a new photographer, we know, but no one will be able to fill your unique 
role here in our hearts. 
At this time, we would like to invite Ed to say a few words. 
Recommended Action:  Distribute certificate   
(AFTER ED SPEAKS, KERRY WILL INTRODUCE HIS VIDEO)

3.   RECOGNITION  
C.   Mina Mori - WSU Center for Peace and Conflict Studies  
Wayne State University's Center for Peace and Conflict Studies canvassed the 
Detroit metropolitan area for educators to study abroad for their educational 
program “Discovering Japan in the 21 st Century: Reconciliation with the 
Past”.  Ten educators were chosen to participate in the project  – Mrs. Mori 
was one of them .  She guided 11 non -Japanese speaking educa tors 
throughout the entire tour and made the trip a great success!  Mrs. Mori went 
above and beyond her duties to assist seven teachers and two board of 
education members for ten days in Japan. Ms. Brenda Carter, President-Elect 
of the Michigan Association of School Boards, asked to join us this evening to 
recognize and honor Mrs. Mori for her contributions to their project.  
Mrs. Carter, would you please come up to say a few words and present Mrs. 
Mori with her certificate?  
Recommended Action:  Invite Brenda Carter up to speak and 
           distribute certificate.   
33
From: Brenda Carter
To: Holmes, Judy
Subject: Re: FW: Mina Mori
Date: Friday, May 27, 2016 10:03:30 PM
Attachments: image003.png
Call-for-Participants-4f.pdf
Good evening Judy,
Wayne State University's Center for Peace and Conflict Studies canvassed the Detroit
metropolitan area for educators to study abroad.  Ten educators, one was Mrs. Mori, were
chosen to participate in the project.  Where I find Mrs. Mori's contribution exceptional is that
she stepped up at the very last minute and guided 11 non-Japanese speaking educators
throughout the entire tour! In my opinion, the trip would have been unsuccessful without her.
I went on this trip to learn how to bring equity in educating the children of Michigan.  As the
incoming president of the Michigan Association of School Boards, I am working hard to
identify what is causing the gap.  One thing that was reiterated over and over again by the
Japanese educators is how highly they value their teachers.  We are unfortunately experiencing
teacher shortages due to the perception that our teachers are not valued.
Mrs. Mori went above and beyond her project to assist seven teachers and two board of
education members through our ten-day stay in Japan. I would like to present Mrs. Mori with a
personal certificate of recognition on June 21st for her contributions to our project. Once
again, I must state that I feel that the project was successful because of Mrs. Mori.
Thank you for allowing me to share this story,
Brenda Carter
President-Elect
Michigan Association of School Boards.
248.521.9632

Good afternoon Nancy,
I am writing you because I would like to give a special presentation for one of
your teachers.  A group of educators recently returned from a ten-day
research project in Japan.  One of these researchers comes from Troy Athens
High School.  What makes this individual exceptional is that she volunteered
at the last minute and saved our year long project.
Our main coordinator took ill.  Mrs. Mori stepped up on very short notice and
guided a group of non-Japanese speaking teachers and educators through

36
Call for Participants!  
Application Due: Sunday, September 30, 2015 
The Center for Peace and Conflict Studies ((http://clas.wayne.edu/cpcs/) at Wayne State 
University, as part of its celebratory 50th Anniversary year, invites high-school teachers and 
educators to join our educational program entitled “Discovering Japan in the 21s Century: 
Reconciliation with the Past.” The program, sponsored by the US-Japan Foundation (http://us-
jf.org/), is presented free of charge to the participants, and will include cultural and informational 
training sessions after school hours and will culminate in a 10-day study tour of Japan in May of 
2016. 
We will select ten participants from the applicants who are interested in learning about present 
day Japan and its efforts to reconcile with the past, with its neighborhoods, and with the United 
States at the 70th anniversary of the end of Asia-Pacific War. The aim is to stimulate educational 
programming for Detroit area students and classrooms; teachers will be asked to devise and 
implement appropriate lesson plans from their experience. 
Requirements: 
1. Be interested in Japan’s historical issues and reconciliation efforts 
2. Be eager to learn about Japanese culture and contemporary Japanese issues 
3. Attend pre-tour sessions including public lectures and other curriculum between October 
2015 and April 2016 
4. Be willing to travel to Japan for the 10 days in May 2016 with permission of your school  
5. Be willing to write a report and teaching manual on Japan’s historical efforts on 
reconciliation with the past 
6. Be willing to collaborate with others  
Duties: 
1. Attend an inaugural event on September 24, 2015 
(https://www.facebook.com/US.JP.CPCS): Open to the Public.  
2. Fully participate in the 7-month bi-weekly training sessions (including public lectures) 
during after-school or weekend hours between October 2015 and April 2016  
3. Prepare a research topic to explore in Japan 
4. Travel to Japan for 10 days in Mid-May 2016 
5. Write a report and teaching manual based on experience and findings, and participate in 
ongoing social media and local district presentation and discussion 
6. Give a presentation on research finding at a Wayne State min-conference in June 2016 
Benefits: 
1. Training and travel support (including airfare, transportation, accommodation, travel 
insurance, and meals in Japan)  
2. Chance to meet Japanese political representatives, diplomats, and scholars  
3. Interaction with peer Japanese high-school teachers and students 
37
Application Process: 
Please submit a two-page MS-word document including the following information to 
ax3845@wayne.edu via email attachment by Sunday, September 30, 2015. 
1. your name and contact address (email, phone, and home address) 
2. Your teaching field and position in your school  
3. Your motivation and interests in joining our program  
4. Your school’s consent to participant in our program including your school principal’s 
name and contact information (email and phone) 
5. Names and contact information (email and phone) for two professional references 
Notification:  
We will send our selection letter to you via an email.  
Questions: 
If you have questions, feel free to contact Dr. Susumu Suzuki, project manager, at 
susumu@wayne.edu.

                     3. D.     06.21.16 
3.       RECOGNITION 
D.  Spirit of Diversity Awards  
The Board of Education is proud to recognize eighteen individuals for our 
Spirit of Diversity Awards. These awards recognize those who help foster a 
climate of inclusion and diversity that creates an optimal learning 
environment for the entire school community. This important work goes on 
throughout the district by our staff, parents, community members and 
students and we are so pleased to be able to honor these recipients tonight.  
This is the fourth year for the Spirit of Diversity Awards, which were 
designed by the Troy Diversity & Inclusion Council as a way to honor those 
who go above and beyond to ensure that every student is valued and 
diversity is celebrated within the Troy School District. When your name is 
read, please come up to receive your certificate and nomination packet.  
Recommended Action:  Kerry Birmingham will read names of the 
winners and the Board will present certificates. 
Morse Diversity and Inclusion Team — Ashourina Yousif, Steph anie 
Zendler, Missy Batts, Jen Bell, Jen Reis, Jen Hilzinger, Meghan Burcham, 
Erin Keyser, Jacklyn Morrison. This group of individuals took on the mission 
of combating hate and educating the community about refugees looking for 
asylum and a new life in the US. They learned about the refugee process in 
the US and in Troy and then worked to combat the stereotypes around this 
vulnerable population. Then they held a ‘Welcome Home’ potluck and the 
fundraiser to give a warm welcome and aid in a fresh start for three 
newcomer families in our area. They also brought the school together in 
the spirit of community and brotherhood, educating one another and 
sharing in a common goal. In addition, Ms. Bell started a program at Morse 
where she will be providing families in the community with potted 
vegetables to take home. Through this initiative, parents will be able to 
provide their families with fresh vegetables throughout the summer. Her 
plans take her all the way into next year where she hopes to create a school 
garden where students and families can pick fresh food to take home. 
Hannah Nagi— Ms. Nay-jee is the sponsor for the Social Justice Project at 
Troy Athens H igh School.  Every year, she  coordinates the Eth nic Fair at 
39
Athens.  Ethnic Fair is an opportunity for students to share cultural traditions 
through food, clothing and performances.  This event brings students, 
parents and the community together to celebrate the diversity in Troy.  
Without Ms. Nagi’s hard work and dedication, this successful annual event 
would not be possible. 
Megan Lubaway, Sarah Bolton, Megan Cunningham— The ECP Tikes 
program is an all inclusive program, wh ere children with developmental 
delays are included in the same environment with children who have met 
their milestones in a typical way. The parents who nominated Ms. Lubaway. 
Ms. Bolton and Ms. Cunningham says this approach is good on both fronts 
because the child with delays has a role model to look up to and the other 
children at a very early age learn mentoring and empathy as they learn not 
all fingers are the same and we all have unique talents that should be valued 
and accepted. One parent goes on to say that these teachers  have been 
very instrumental in the development of her child, who she has seen grow 
from an introverted child with speech delays  to a child who is learning to 
use her voice and stand  up for herself.  Both parents say these  same 
sentiments are reflected by all the kids and parents of the  classroom and 
they truly appreciates the efforts of these three teachers, who go above 
and beyond to make this happen. 
Angelina Lai—Her nominator says Mrs. Lai  has made a great impact at 
Morse Elementary, where we have had a number of families move to their 
school from China.  Angelina has given her time to work very closely with 
these families to translate for various meetings.  She also took the initiative 
to help the families learn more about American schools and the Ameri can 
culture. She has worked with the ELD team at Morse to host “bilingual 
coffee meetings” where the families can have open discussions. The 
outcome of Angelina’s efforts has been very apparent. Our Chinese families 
have felt very welcomed into our communi ty. They participate in school 
events, attend conferences, and they even planned a Chinese Ne w Year 
assembly for our school. In addition, Angelina was part of a professional 
development for teachers and staff at Morse. She sat on a panel with other 
parents and was open to answering any questions about the Chinese 
culture.  
Raje and Rahul Angamuthu— Rajee & Rahool  Ang-a-mutu emanate the 
core values of the Troy School District. They demonstrate this through their 
commitment to many Diversity & Inclusion initiatives including Beyond Black 
& White, the water drive for the Flint community, helping with the Diversity 
& Inclusion video, as well as offering support and connections beyond what 
the district provides. Their joint leadership is inspiring to mothers and sons 
40
everywhere and we are grateful for their passion and their willingness to 
serve our community. As their nominator wrote, t heir dedicated 
commitment to create more just and equitable learning environments is 
clear from the minute you meet them.   
Erin Johnson— Ms. Johnson holds weekly meetings of life club at Boulan 
Park Middle School and has opened it up to a diverse group of students to 
come and discuss things that are of concern to them.  She has found a way 
to connect with students through real life experiences and she provides 
support for t he students throughout the day. The parent who nominated 
Ms. Johnson says her son has described how she has supported him when 
he is frustrated and that she advocates for him when needed.  The parent 
goes on to say Ms. Johnson  has conveyed a genuine interest in a diverse 
group of students and has helped when they have ne eded support.  He r 
son describes her as the best teacher that he has had at Boulan. 
Danielle Andresen—Danielle Andresen is described as a tireless advocate 
for low income, newcomer families that far too often fall through the cracks, 
even in communities like ours that see and value the huge benefits our 
diversity brings. Ms. Andresen provides a role model for other educators 
and folks wanting to build community, often with shrinking budgets. Her 
passion to serve the families new to our area has been vital  for us in 
reimagining resources and education in our changing community.  Her 
nominator says Ms. Andresen sees not more than just a student new to a 
school, she see s a family new to a community. She constantly looks for 
ways to engage families in the educa tional community for the expressed 
benefit of all of us. She has partnered with organizations like the Nature 
Center to provide resources for those ‘gaps’ in our newcomer student 
experiences and information.  She has created a series of hands -on nights 
for newcomer families at the Nature Center to learn together about local 
Michigan wildlife; a topic explored typically through experiences in early 
elementary often missed by kids coming later on. Simply put, Ms. Andresen 
brings people together in a way that benefits all of us. 
On behalf of the Board of Education, we thank all of these wonderful 
Diversity Champions for their wonderful work in our TSD community.

                     3. E.     06.21.16 
3.       RECOGNITION 
E.  NSPRA Superintendent to Watch—Dr. Richard Machesky  
We are proud to recognize Troy School District Superintendent Dr. Richard 
Machesky, who was named a 2016 “Superintendent  to Watch ” by the 
National Schools Public Relations Association. This prestigious program 
recognizes less than 25 superintendents across the nation for their dynamic, 
fast-paced leadership with strong communication at its core. The leaders 
honored in this way engage and inform their school communities with new 
communication technology tools combined with tried -and-true techniques. 
The judges praised Dr. Machesky’s commitment to effective communication 
and his understand ing that it leads to school and student success . They 
were impressed by his willingness to expand two-way communication and 
outreach efforts and his OneTROY message of unity, value and inclusion of 
all. 
Dr. Machesky is one of a select group to receive this honor this year. He 
was previously named the Michigan Outstanding Superintendent 
Communicator of the Year and will be formally named with this new 
recognition at NSPRA’s national conference in Chicago in July. 
Recommended Action:  Present award.

TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
Troy, Michigan 
Superintendent of Schools Employment Agreement  
W I T N E S S E T H 
 WHEREAS, the TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT is a duly organized School District, 
and its Board is authorized to employ a Superintendent; and, 
 WHEREAS, Dr. Richard M. Machesky is desirous of being employed by the 
Board as Superintendent of Schools in accordance with the terms hereof; 
 NOW, THEREFORE,  in consideration of the mutual covenants hereinafter 
set forth, the parties agree as follows:   
This Employment Agreement (hereinafter referred to as “Agreement”) 
entered into on June 21, 2016  is between the Board of Education (hereinafter 
referred to as the “Board”) of the Troy School District (hereinafter referred to as 
the “District”) ,” and Dr. Richard M. Machesk y (hereinafter referred to as the 
“Superintendent”). 
 1. EMPLOYMENT TERM - The Board agrees to employ Superintendent for 
the period commencing on July 1, 2016  and terminating on June 30, 2019  
subject to the provisions of this Agreement.  This Agreement may be renewed at 
any time prior  to its expiration date of June 30, 2019 by a duly authorized 
written agreement between the Board and the Superintendent.   
 2. QUALIFICATIONS - The Superintendent shall possess and maintain, 
throughout the duration of this Agr eement, the educational qualifications 
required by Section 1246 of the Michigan School Code of 1976 (Public Act 451of 
148
1976), as may be amended from time to time, and/or otherwise as required by 
law.  
3. DUTIES AND RESPONSIBILITIES - The Superintendent agrees to serve the 
District and perform the duties in his capacity as a Superintendent as directed by 
the Board and as required by the laws of the state of Michigan.  The 
Superintendent further agrees to obey and fulfill the policies, rules and 
regulations as established from time to time by the Board and to carry out its 
programs and policies during the entire term of this Agreement.   
4. PERFORMANCE EVALUATION – The Board shall provide the 
Superintendent with a written evaluation of his performance no later than June 1 
of each calendar year.  The instrument and evaluation format used by the Board 
shall be made known to the Superintendent by November 1 of the immediately 
prior calendar year. 
 5. SALARY - The Superintendent shall be paid an annual salary of One 
Hundred Eighty-four, Two Hundred Thirty Dollars ($184,230).  The Board retains 
the right to adjust the Superintendent’s annual salary during the continuation of 
this Agreement on a prospective basis only, but such adjustment shall not 
reduce the annual salary below the figure specified in this paragraph.   
6. WORK YEAR - The Superintendent shall perform his duties over the 
full fifty-two weeks (52 -weeks) of the District’s fiscal year (July 1 through June 
30), less applicable vacation, leave, and holidays.  The Superintendent shall be 
expected to attend meetings of the Board and its committees and to attend and 
participate in District functions or, on occasion, other civic activities having 
relation to the District’s interests within the Troy community.  The  time 
 2 
149
expended in attending such meetings and activities has been taken into account 
in setting the aforesaid salary and thus no additional compensation shall be 
forthcoming for such attendances. 
7.  FRINGE BENEFITS - During the term of this Agreement the 
Superintendent shall receive the fringe benefits set forth in the Fringe Benefit 
Supplement attached to this Agreement and made a part hereof.  Fringe benefits 
shall be subject to an annual review for modification by the Board. 
8. OUTSIDE EMPLOYMENT - The Superintendent agrees not to engage in 
other employment or self -employment during the term of the contract without 
the prior consent of the Board. 
9. CONFLICT OF INTEREST – The Superintendent will faithfully serve the 
District and be regardful of its inter est during the term of this Agreement, and 
thereafter to the extent required by this Agreement and by law.  The 
Superintendent will not directly or indirectly acquire or otherwise possess any 
interest adverse to that of the District.  In the event that a q uestion arises as to 
whether a given interest is in conflict with the interests of the District, the 
Superintendent shall make a full disclosure of same to the Board for their review 
and disposition, which disposition shall be controlling and complied with  by the 
Superintendent.   
The Superintendent represents that he does not directly or indirectly 
possess any interest adverse to that of the District. 
 10. RESIDENCY – The Board prefers that the Superintendent be a resident 
in the District, however, the parties understand that it is not required.  
 3 
150
11. DISCIPLINE & TERMINATION– The Superintendent may be disciplined, 
suspended, or terminated at any time during the term of this Agreement for 
reasonable and good cause .  In the event of discipline or termination,  the 
Superintendent shall receive notice and a fair hearing.   
In addition to any other rights the District may have by law or under this 
Agreement, the term “good cause” shall include, without limitation, an act of 
moral turpitude, any misdemeanor or felo ny criminal conviction or plea of nolo 
contendre, violation of this Agreement or disability as provided for in Paragraph 
F. of the Fringe Benefit Supplement.  In the event of a claim of violation of this 
Agreement, the Board shall give the Superintendent t hirty days (30 days) written 
notice and opportunity to cure any curable violation, as well as the opportunity 
to attend any Board meeting at which a vote is taken on the issue of termination 
of employment.  Upon the discharge of the Superintendent, this Agreement shall 
be terminated.     
12. ARBITRATION - The Board and the Superintendent agree to arbitrate 
any and all disputes arising out of this contract under the Employment Rules of 
the American Arbitration Association.  Any arbitration shall be conducted  in 
Oakland County, Michigan.  The parties shall equally share the cost of the 
arbitrator’s fees and any hearing room rental fees.  Any award or decision arising 
out of such arbitration shall be enforceable in a court of competent jurisdiction 
in the state  of Michigan.  In the event of an arbitration arising out of a 
termination of this Agreement by the Board, the arbitrator’s opinion shall be 
limited to determining whether the Board had good cause under the terms of 
this contract for terminating the Superintendent’s employment.   
 4 
151
13.  NON-RENEWAL OF AGREEMENT – The decision whether to renew or not to 
renew the Agreement with or without cause is solely within the discretion of the 
Board. However, in the event that the Board decides not to renew this 
Agreement, prior written notice of such non -renewal shall be given to the 
Superintendent at least ninety days (90-days) prior to its expiration date. 
 The Superintendent may terminate this Agreement by providing at least a 
ninety- day (90-day) written notice to th e Board. At its option, the Board may 
waive all or part of the requirement for the Superintendent to give the Board 
notice. 
 14. TENURE EXCLUSION – It is expressly agreed that the Superintendent 
shall not be deemed to be granted continuing tenure under the  Teachers’ 
Tenure Act, Act 4 of the Public Acts of 1937 (Extra Session), as amended, or any 
other applicable statutes in the capacity of Superintendent, or any other 
administrative capacity by virtue of this Agreement, or any extension or renewal 
hereof. 
15. MEDICAL EXAMINATION – Annually, the Superintendent shall have a 
medical examination.  A written statement from the examining licensed medical 
physician certifying the fitness of the Superintendent for the essential duties of 
his position shall be provid ed to the President of the Board as soon as it is 
available following the examination.   Physical examination costs not covered by 
insurance will be reimbursed to the Superintendent.  The Board reserves the 
right to direct the Superintendent to an examining physician for a medical report 
if, in its judgment, there are circumstances which warrant a verification of either 
the physical or psychological competence of the Superintendent.  The reports 
 5 
152
from both the annual examination and the latter examination sh all be shared by 
the Board President with the other trustees, but shall otherwise be maintained in 
confidence. 
16. INDEMNIFICATION.  The Board agrees that it shall defend, hold 
harmless and indemnify the Superintendent from any and all demands, claims, 
suits, administrative proceedings, actions, judgments, damages, liabilities 
and/or legal proceedings (including attorney fees, costs and expenses ) brought 
against the Superintendent in his individual capacity or in his official capacity as 
agent and employee of the District.  The Board agrees to indemnify the 
Superintendent as stated herein, provided the incident arose while the 
Superintendent was acting within the scope of his employment and excluding 
criminal proceedings at the sole discretion of the Board, and claims or actions of 
the District.  It is understood that the indemnity given shall not extend to any 
demands, claims, suits, administrative proceedings, actions, judgments, 
damages, liabilities and/or legal proceedings arising out of the alleged 
intentional tortious actions or inactions (as opposed, for example, to alleged 
acts of negligence) of the Superintendent.  The Superintendent shall give the 
Board notice of the nature of any claim of indemnification hereunder promptly 
upon receipt of notice of any claim or action.  The Board shall have the right to 
conduct the defense of any such claim and Superintendent shall fully cooperate 
with the Board in defense.  The Superintendent may, at his own cost and 
expense, employ counsel to assist in such defense .  The Board shall provide 
public liability insurance at a reasonable cost for the Superintendent to cover 
legal expenses in defense of claims and payment of judgments resulting from 
 6 
153
his functioning as Superintendent and will reimburse him for any portion of such 
expense and judgments not covered by insurance.  In no case will individual 
Board members be considered personally liable for indemnifying the 
Superintendent against such demands, claims, suits, actions and legal 
proceedings. 
 17. ENTIRE AGREEMENT - This Agreement and the Fringe Benefit 
Supplement contains all of the terms agreed to by the parties with respect to the 
subject matter of this Agreement and supersedes all prior agreements, 
arrangements and communications between the parties concerning s uch subject 
matter, whether oral or written.  Without limiting the generality of the foregoing, 
it is specifically understood and agreed that the Administrative Handbook of the 
Troy School District is not applicable to the Superintendent, and that no part of 
said Handbook is in this Agreement between the Superintendent and the Board. 
 18. APPLICABLE LAW - This contract is subject in all respects to all of the

accordance with the laws of the state of Michigan.  If any provision or segment of 
this Agreement shall be determined by a court of competent jurisdiction to be 
unlawful and/or unenforceable, then such provision or segment shall be deemed 
to be severed from the remainder of the Ag reement and the Agreement shall 
otherwise be in full force and effect.

FRINGE BENEFIT SUPPLEMENT 
A. HEALTH, DENTAL, AND VISION INSURANCE – The Board shall provide the 
Superintendent the same single, two -person, or full family insurance on the 
same basis as it provides said insurances to other administrators in the District.  
The Superintendent ackn owledges that the insurance provisions for 
administrators may be modified or changed from time to time, and that such 
modifications or changes will correspondingly alter the insurance benefits in this 
paragraph.   
B. LONG-TERM DISABILITY INSURANCE - The B oard shall provide the 
Superintendent with a long -term disability insurance plan on the same basis it 
provides said insurance to other administrators in the District.  Such long -term 
disability insurance shall provide benefits of sixty-six and two-thirds percent (66-
2/3%) of the monthly earnings to the Superintendent who is unable to work due 
to extended sickness or injury.  These benefits shall be payable until the 
Superintendent reaches age seventy (70). 
For the purpose of long -term disability coverage, monthly earnings shall include 
the Superintendent’s compensation subject to Board -paid retirement 
contributions to the Michigan Public School Employees Retirement System 
(MPSERS) at the time of disability divided by twelve (12) months.  The long -term 
disability coverage shall have a one hundred-eighty (180) day waiting period. 
The amount received from the insurance company will be reduced by any primary 
remuneration received during the benefit period from the Board, the Michigan 
Public School Employee’s Re tirement Fund, and the Federal Social Security Act, 
and is subject to such other provisions of the long -term disability policy 
currently in effect. 
C. LIFE INSURANCE - The Board shall provide the Superintendent a group 
term life insurance policy on the sa me basis that it provides said insurance to 
other administrators in the District that shall pay a sum equal to  three (3) times 
annual earnings up to a maximum of $650,000 payable to the Superintendent’s 
designated beneficiary at the time of death. 
D. ACCIDENTAL DEATH AND DISMEMBERMENT INSURANCE - The Board shall 
provide the Superintendent with a group  accidental death and dismemberment 
insurance policy on the same basis as it provides said insurance to other 
administrators in the District that shall pay a sum equal to three (3) times annual 
earnings up to a maximum of $650,000 payable to the Superintendent’s 
designated beneficiary at the time of death. 
E. WORKER’S COMPENSATION  - In the event of any on -the-job injury, the 
Superintendent must notify the business office as soon as possible after the 
occurrence.  He shall be required to execute and submit to the business office 
all forms necessary in connection with such injury.  The business office shall 
make any necessary determinations concerning the on-the-job injury. 
 9 
156
In the event the Superintendent is absent from work due to a job -related 
accident, the Superintendent will be paid, for a period not to exceed sixty days 
(60-days) from the date of the accident, the difference between the 
Superintendent’s ful l salary and such monies as may be received from the 
Worker’s Compensation benefits. 
Any benefits beyond the entry into the long -term disability insurance coverage 
shall be payable only under the terms of the Worker’s Compensation Act and 
long-term disability insurance coverage of the District.  No District supplement 
will be made after entry into the long-term disability coverage. 
F.   INCOME PROTECTION - During the term of this contract, the 
Superintendent’s salary will be continued by the District for any period of 
temporary incapacity due to illness or other disability but not beyond any 
continuous period of one hundred and eighty (180) calendar days .  (Any 
amounts received by Superintendent through Board -paid disability insurance or 
statutory Worker’s Compensation will be credited against this salary obligation.)  
At any time the Superintendent has been incapacitated by illness or otherwise 
for a continu ous period exceeding twelve (12)  calendar months, the Board may 
terminate this Agreement by furnishi ng written notice of termination to the 
Superintendent.  Thereafter, the Superintendent shall be entitled to continue 
insurance benefits for the remainder of the first year period that he receives 
long-term disability benefits.  The Superintendent shall be  deemed eligible to 
continue long-term disability insurance benefits for the period of disability as if 
he were still employed by the District, provided he meets the other eligibility 
requirements under the applicable insurance policy.  As used herein, the  term 
“incapacity” means inability to perform full -time duties as Superintendent.  The 
District reserves the right to require satisfactory proof of any such incapacity 
and, in this connection, to have the Superintendent examined by a physician of 
its choice and at its expense. 
G.   ProfESSIONAL DUES AND DEVELOPMENT - The Board shall provide 
payment for one state, one local and one national association dues selection of 
the Superintendent’s discretion. The Superintendent, with prior Board approval, 
shall be eligible annually to attend any national recognized executive leadership 
conference that relates to his role as Superintendent.  Further, the 
Superintendent shall be reimbursed tuition and registration fees for required 
professional development activities or leadership conferences on the same basis 
as other employees are reimbursed. 
H.   BUSINESS EXPENSES – The Superintendent is eligible to receive 
reimbursement for reasonable and appropriate business -related expenses  in 
accordance with the Internal Reven ue Code, Internal Revenue Guidelines, 
Guidelines established by the Michigan Department of Treasury, and provided 
that valid receipts and expense reports are provided to the District. 
 10 
157
I. VACATION DAYS - The Superintendent shall have  25 paid vacation 
days per fiscal year, which may not be carried forward from year to year.   
In the event of termination of this Agreement prior to the expiration 
thereof, or in the event of non -renewal of this Agreement, the Board agrees to 
pay the Superintendent for the prorated unused earned vacation time at his pro-
rata daily rate (Salary, as identified in paragraph 5 ÷ 365 days)  specified for the 
fiscal year in which said payment is made . For purposes of this Section, the 
following formula shall apply: 
Number of Total Vac ation Days ÷ 12 x Number of months worked minus used 
vacation time equals the prorated unused earned vacation time. 
J. HOLIDAYS – The Superintendent shall have twelve (12) paid holidays.  
The paid holidays are the following:  July 4, Labor Day, Thanksgivi ng Day, Friday 
after Thanksgiving, Christmas Eve, Christmas Day, New Year’s Eve, New Year’s 
Day, Good Friday, Memorial Day, and two floating holidays during the winter 
recess.  Pay for these holidays is included in the Superintendent’s salary. 
K.  TAX-DEFERRED ANNUITY-  As added compensation,  the Board may 
provide Superintendent with a tax -sheltered annuity .  The Superintendent may 
direct the investment of said funds to an approved carrier in the District.   
M. PERFORMANCE BASED PAY- The Superintendent sh all be eligible to 
receive performance based pay, consistent with the applicable Board Policy and 
Administrative Procedures, if any.  The Superintendent acknowledges that the 
district retains the right to amend any performance -based pay plan, which 
amendment, if any, shall apply to the Superintendent.  
N. MINIMUM DISTRICT FUND BALANCE-  It is expressly agreed upon that 
in the event the Board of Education’s year end audited fund balance drops below 
12% in any given year, the Board of Education reserves the right to make 
unilateral reductions in total compensation effective the succeeding January 1 st 
of the given year so long as the reduction made to this contract is consistent 
with the percentage reductions required by other bargaining units or classes of 
employee groups within the District.  
END OF FRINGE BENEFIT SUPPLEMENT

EMPLOYMENT CONTRACT 
 This Agreement is made between the Troy School District, a public body 
corporate located in the county of Oakland, state of Michigan (herein called the 
"District"), acting through its Board of Education, and Mark Dziatczak  of Oakland 
County, Michigan (herein called "Deputy Superintendent") as follows: 
 1. Term of Employment  - The Distric t hereby appoints  Mark Dziatczak  as 
Deputy Superintendent for Teaching and Learning for the term commencing July 1, 2016, 
and ending June 30, 2018, unless this contract is sooner terminated as herein provided. 
 2.1 Annual Salary  - For services rendered pursuant to this contract, the 
District agrees to pay Deputy Superintendent an annual (fifty-two week) salary at the rate 
of one hundred forty -five thousand dollars ($ 145,000).  In the absence of mutual 
agreement on a salary and benefits increase and/or a contract extension, Deputy 
Superintendent's salary and benefits and the term of the contract shall remain as stated 
herein or as stated in the most recent mutual written agreement of the parties. 
 2.2 Board-funded, Tax -sheltered Annuity  – The District agrees to provide 
Deputy Superintendent with a board -funded, tax-sheltered annuity of three  (3%) percent 
of the base salary for the 2016-2017 and 201 7 – 2018 school year s.  This amount is 
intended as additional compensation and/or remuneration for services performed by the 
Deputy Superintendent. 
 3. Evaluation - The District agrees that Deputy Superintendent will be 
evaluated by the Superintendent annually and will receive a written copy of each annual 
evaluation not later than June first prior to the conclusion of each school year this 
contract is in effect.  After the receipt of the written evaluation, Deputy Superintendent 
shall be given an opportunity to mee t with the Superintendent to d iscuss the evaluation.  
160
If the S uperintendent considers Deputy Superintendent's performance unsatisfactory in 
any respect, he/she will specifically identify any shortcoming(s) in the written evaluation. 
 4. Additional Benefits  - During the life of this contract Deputy 
Superintendent will receive fringe benefits as specified on Schedule A attached hereto. 
 5. Reimbursement for Expenses - Deputy Superintendent shall be reimbursed 
for reasonable and necessary expenses incurred for  or on behalf of the District, in 
connection with meetings with students, staff employees, visitors, etc.  Deputy 
Superintendent shall be reimbursed for dues in connection with appropriate professional 
and local civic and community organizations and for ex penses incurred in attendance at 
appropriate educational and professional meetings at the local, state and national level.  
Deputy Superintendent shall submit p roper vouchers together with his/her request for 
reimbursement in accordance with District policy. 
 6. Termination in Event of Death  - This contract shall be automatically 
terminated in the event of the death of Deputy Superintendent. 
 7. Termination for Cause  - This contract may be terminated by District for 
willful violation by Deputy Superintendent of any rules, regulations, directives or policies 
of the Board of Education of the District, violation of any of the terms of this contract, or 
for legal and just cause, including, but not limited to, misconduct or lack of legal 
qualifications. 
 8. Salary Rate on Termination Resulting From Death or Cause  - In the event 
of termination of this contr act as set forth in paragraphs 6 or 7  above, Deputy 
Superintendent shall be entitled to salary and benefits only for services rendered to the 
date of termination at the salary rate then in effect. 
 9. Deputy Superintendent's Duties  - Deputy Superintendent agrees that 
during the term of this contract he will perform competently and diligently the duties of 
161
the office of Deputy Superintendent as prescribed by the Board of Education and the laws 
of the State of Michigan, and that he will faithfully observe and implement the policies, 
rules, regulations and directives properly adopted and issued by said Board for the 
government of the District. 
 10. Attendance at Board Meetings - Deputy Superintendent, if physically able, 
may be directed to attend any regular meetings and special meetings of the Board of 
Education. 
 11. Indemnification of Deputy Superintendent - The Board agrees to 
indemnify and hold Deputy Superintendent harmless from and against all claims, suits or 
administrative proceedings, judgments, damages, liabilities, (including attorney fees, 
costs and expenses) asserted by any third party against Deputy Superintendent arising 
from actions taken or decisions made within the scope of his employment during the term 
of this contract notwithstanding that said claims may be asserted after the termination of 
this Contract; provided, however, that the indemnity given hereby shall not extend to any 
claims, demands, suits or administrative proceedings arising out of the alleged intentional 
tortious actions or inactions (as opposed, for example, to alleged acts of negligence) of 
Deputy Superintendent.  Deputy Superintendent shall give the Board notice of the nature 
of any claim of indemnification hereunder promptly upon receipt of notice of any claim 
or action.  The Board shall have the right to conduct the defense of any such claim and 
Deputy Superintendent shall fully cooperate with the Board in the defense.  Deputy 
Superintendent may, at Deputy Superintendent's own cost and expense, employ counsel 
to assist in such defense. 
 12. Exclusion of Tenure in Administrative Capacity  and Non -Renewal - 
Deputy Superintendent shall not be deemed to be granted continuing tenure in th e 
capacity of Deputy Superintendent or in any other administrative capacity by virtue of 
162
this contract of employment or otherwise.  The decision whether to renew or not to renew 
the Agreement with or without cause is solely within the discretion of the Boa rd. 
However, in the event that the Board decides not to renew this Agreement, prior written 
notice of such non -renewal shall be given to the Deputy Superintendent at least ninety 
days (90-days) prior to its expiration date. 
 13. Entire Agreement  - This con tract contains the entire agreement between 
the parties, and there are no other representations, promises or agreements between them 
whether oral, written, or inferred.  Without limiting the generality of the foregoing, it is 
specifically understood and agreed that the Administrative Handbook of the Troy School 
District is not applicable to Deputy Superintendent and that no part of said Handbook is 
any of this contract between Deputy Superintendent and the District. 
 14. Applicable Law - This contract is su bject in all respects to all of the laws 
of the state of Michigan, and shall be governed by and interpreted in accordance with the 
laws of the state of Michigan. 
15.  Arbitration- The Board and the Assistant Superintendent agree to arbitrate any 
and all disputes arising out of this contract under the Employment Rules of the American 
Arbitration Association.  Any arbitration shall be conducted in Oakland County, 
Michigan.  The parties shall equally share the cost of the arbitrator’s fees and any hearing 
room rental fees.  Any award or decision arising out of such arbitration shall be 
enforceable in a court of competent jurisdiction in the state of Michigan.  In the event of 
an arbitration arising out of a termination of this Agreement by the Board, the arbitr ator’s 
opinion shall be limited to determining whether the Board had just cause under the terms 
of this contract for terminating the Assistant Superintendent’s employment.

employee insurance premium contributions for the coverage elected as

  Option to Medical Insurance Coverage: Deputy Superintendent m ay

165
 (c) Vision Insurance .  The Deputy Superintendent will be provided vision 
insurance coverage on the same basis as the other administrators in the 
District. 
 (d)  Group Term Life Insurance.  The Deputy Superintendent will be provided 
group term life insurance in the amount of three (3) times earnings, up to a 
maximum of five hundred -thousand dollars ($500,000) payable to the 
Deputy Superintendent’s designated beneficiary at the time of death. 
  Further, accidental death and dismemberment insur ance will pay to the

Deputy Superintendent shall be paid according to the schedule of benefits 
for other covered losses. 
 (e) Worker’s Compensation Insurance.   Worker’s compensation benefits will 
be provided as required by applicable law. 
 (f) Long-term Disability Insurance .  Upon employment and after starting 
work, the Deputy Superintendent is granted up to 180 calendar days  of 
income protection for personal illness or disability, w hich exceeds five 
consecutive workdays in duration.  Said benefits shall be: 1) as provided 
to other administrators; 2) reduced by any primary remuneration rec eived 
during the benefit period from the District, the Michigan Public School 
Employees Retirement System and/or the Federal Social Security System; 
and 3) according to the terms and conditions of the policy in effect that is 
purchased by the District.  The rate of pay for purposes of income 
protection shall be the Deputy Superintendent’s rate of pay on the first day 
of illness or disability.  After expiration of the one hundred and eighty 
(180) days of income protection, the Deputy Superintendent will receive 
166
long-term disability payments, provided the Deputy Superintendent 
qualifies under the terms of the long-term disability insurance policy. 
   If the Deputy Superintendent incurs a second unrelated personal 
illness or unrelated disability the Deputy Superintendent will re-qualify for 
up to one hundred and eighty (180) days of income protection provi ded 
the Deputy Superintendent has worked at least one hundred and eighty 
(180) days between periods of illness or disability. 
  During any period of disability , the Deputy Superintendent will 
receive the insurance benefits provided in this contract  for two years (24 
months) subject to any required employee contributions under Public Act 
152 of 2011.  
  (NOTE:  With regard to all of the above insurance and any insurance 
which may hereafter be provided to the Deputy Superintendent at District

and the carrier’s rules and regulations shall govern as to all the specifics of 
coverage, including but not limited to such matters as commencement, 
duration and amounts of benefits and eligibility or qualification for 
benefits.) 
(2) VACATION DAYS.  Deputy Superintendent will be entitled to twenty -eight (28) 
vacation days per school year, of which 10 can be carried forward to the next 
school year.  Deputy Superintendent may cash out a maximum of three (3) unused 
vacation days at the end of the fiscal year.

(3) HOLIDAYS.  Holidays are Labor Day, Thanksgivi ng Day and the day following, 
Christmas plus one day, New Year's plus one day, Good Friday, Memorial Day, 
July 4, and two additional days during the winter holiday recess period.  Pay for 
these holidays is included in the Deputy Superintendent's salary. 
(4) JURY DUTY.  If required to perform jury duty, Deputy Superintendent will be 
paid the difference between Deputy Superintendent's daily salary and the daily 
jury fee paid by the court (not including travel allowance or reimbursement of 
expenses) for each d ay on which Deputy Superintendent reports for or performs 
jury duty. 
 (5) SEVERANCE PAY.  Deputy Superintendent shall be eligible to receive

other administrators in the Distric t hired on or after July 1, 1987.  Deputy 
Superintendent acknowledges that the District retains the right to amend any

Superintendent.

amendment, if any, shall apply to Deputy Superintendent.

modified or changed from time to time, and that such modifications or 
changes will correspondingly alter the insurance benefits in this paragraph. 
  Option to Medical Insurance Coverage: Assistant Superintendent may 
choose not to be covered by a Board-paid medical insurance plan and elect 
to receive the same cash payment as provided to administrators  in the 
Administrative Handbook annually.  The District makes no representation 
regarding the tax consequences of this paragraph.   
175
 (c) Vision Insurance .  The Assistant Superintendent will be provided vision 
insurance coverage on the same basis as the oth er administrators in the 
District. 
 (d)  Group Term Life Insurance .  The Assistant Superintendent will be 
provided group term life insurance in the amount of three (3) times 
earnings, up to a maximum of five hundred -thousand dollars ($500,000) 
payable to t he Assistant Superintendent’s designated beneficiary at the 
time of death. 
  Further, accidental death and dismemberment insurance will pay to the 
designated beneficiary a sum equal to  three (3) times  earnings for 
accidental death.  Assistant Superintenden t shall be paid according to the 
schedule of benefits for other covered losses. 
 (e) Worker’s Compensation Insurance.   Worker’s compensation benefits will 
be provided as required by applicable law. 
 (f) Long-term Disability Insurance .  Upon employment and after starting 
work, the Assistant Superintendent is granted up to 180 calendar days of 
income protection for personal illness or disability, which exceeds five 
consecutive workdays in duration.  Said benefits shall be: 1) as provided 
to other administrato rs; 2) reduced by any primary remuneration rec eived 
during the benefit period from the District, the Michigan Public School 
Employees Retirement System and/or the Federal Social Security System; 
and 3) according to the terms and conditions of the policy in  effect that is 
purchased by the District.  The rate of pay for purposes of income 
protection shall be the Assistant Superintendent’s rate of pay on the first 
day of illness or disability.  After expiration of the one hundred and eighty 
176
(180) days of income protection, the Assistant Superintendent will receive 
long-term disability payments, provided the Assistant Superintendent 
qualifies under the terms of the long-term disability insurance policy. 
   If the Assistant Superintendent incurs a second unrelate d personal 
illness or unrelated disability the Assistant Superintendent will re -qualify 
for up to one hundred and eighty (180) days of income protection provided 
the Assistant Superintendent has worked at least one hundred and eighty 
(180) days between periods of illness or disability. 
  During any period of disability , the Assistant Superintendent will 
receive the insurance benefits provided in this contract  for two years (24 
months) subject to any required employee contributions under Public Act 
152 of 2011.  
  (NOTE:  With regard to all of the above insurance and any insurance 
which may hereafter be provided to the Assistant Superintendent at 
District expense: it is understood and agreed that the District’s only 
obligation shall be to pay the premiums for  insurance policies (which may 
be group policies) as generally described above, and that the terms of such 
policies and the carrier’s rules and regulations shall govern as to all the 
specifics of coverage, including but not limited to such matters as 
commencement, duration and amounts of benefits and eligibility or 
qualification for benefits.) 
(2) VACATION DAYS.  Assistant Superintendent will be entitled to twenty -eight 
(28) vacation days per school year, of which 10 can be carried forward to the next 
school year.  Assistant Superintendent may cash out a maximum of three  (3) 
unused vacation days at the end of the fiscal year.   
177
(3) HOLIDAYS.  Holidays are Labor Day, Thanksgiving Day and the day following, 
Christmas plus one day, New Year's plus one day, Good  Friday, Memorial Day, 
July 4, and two additional days during the winter holiday recess period.  Pay for 
these holidays is included in the Assistant Superintendent's salary. 
(4) JURY DUTY.  If required to perform jury duty, Assistant Superintendent will be  
paid the difference between Assistant Superintendent's daily salary and the daily 
jury fee paid by the court (not including travel allowance or reimbursement of 
expenses) for each day on which Assistant Superintendent reports for or performs 
jury duty. 
 (5) SEVERANCE PAY.  Assistant Superintendent shall be eligible to receive 
severance pay on the same basis as other administrators, if any is available to 
other administrators in the District  hired on or after July 1, 1987.  Assistant 
Superintendent acknowle dges that the District retains the right to amend any 
severance pay plan, which amendment, if any, shall apply to Assistant 
Superintendent. 
(6) IRS SECTION 125 PLAN.  If a Section 125 account is  established for 
administrators covered by the Administrative Handbook, Assistant Superintendent 
shall receive same. 
(7) PERFORMANCE-BASED PAY.  Assistant Superintendent shall be eligible to 
receive performance-based pay, consistent with the applicable Board Policy and 
Administrative Procedures, if any.  Assistant Su perintendent acknowledges that 
the district retains the right to amend any performance -based pay plan, which 
amendment, if any, shall apply to Assistant Superintendent.  
178
(8)  IRS SECTION 125 PLAN.  If a Section 125 account is established for 
administrators covered by the Administrative Handbook, Assistant Superintendent 
shall receive same. 
(9)  MINIMUM BOARD OF EDUCATION FUND BALANCE .  It is expressly 
agreed upon that in the event the Board of Education’s year end audited fund 
balance drops below 12% in any  given year, the Board of Education reserves the 
right to make unilateral reductions in total compensation effective the succeeding 
January 1 st of the given year so long as the reduction made to this contract is 
consistent with the percentage reductions re quired by other bargaining units or 
classes of employee groups within the District.  
    END OF SCHEDULE A

EMPLOYMENT CONTRACT 
 This Agreement is made between the Troy School District, a public body 
corporate located in the county of Oakland, state of Michigan (herein called the 
"District"), acting through its Board of Education, and Kristine Griffor  of St. Cl air 
County, Michigan (herein called "Assistant Superintendent") as follows: 
 1. Term of Employment  - The Distric t hereby appoints  Kristine Griffor  as 
Assistant Superintendent of  Elementary Curriculum and Instruction for the term 
commencing July 1, 2016 , an d ending June 30, 20 18, unless this contract is sooner 
terminated as herein provided. 
 2.1 Annual Salary  - For services rendered pursuant to this contract, the 
District agrees to pay Assistant Superintendent a n annual (fifty -two week) salary at the 
rate of one hundred thirty -five thousand dollars ($135,000) commencing with the 2016 -
17 school year and one hundred forty thousand dollars ($140,000) commencing with the 
2017-18 school year .  In the absence of mutual agreement on a salary and benefits 
increase and/or a contract extension, Assistant Superintendent's salary and benefits and 
the term of the contract shall remain as stated herein or as stated in the most recent 
mutual written agreement of the parties. 
 2.2 Board-funded, Tax -sheltered Annuity  – The Dis trict agrees to provide 
Assistant Superintendent with a board -funded, tax -sheltered annuity of three  (3%) 
percent of the base salary for the 2016-2017 and 2017 – 2018 school years.  This amount 
is intended as additional compensation and/or remuneration for services performed by the 
Assistant Superintendent. 
 3. Evaluation - The District agrees that Assistant Superintendent will be 
evaluated by the Superintendent annually and will receive a written copy of each annual 
180
evaluation not later than June first pri or to the conclusion of each school year this 
contract is in effect.  After the receipt of the written evaluation, Assistant Superintendent 
shall be given an opportunity to meet with the Superintendent to d iscuss the evaluation.  
If the S uperintendent considers Assistant Superintendent's performance unsatisfactory in 
any respect, he/she will specifically identify any shortcoming(s) in the written evaluation. 
 4. Additional Benefits  - During the life of this contract Assistant 
Superintendent will receive fringe benefits as specified on Schedule A attached hereto. 
 5. Reimbursement for Expenses  - Assistant Superintendent shall be 
reimbursed for reasonable and necessary expenses incurred for or on behalf of the 
District, in connection with meetings with student s, staff employees, visitors, etc.  
Assistant Superintendent shall be reimbursed for dues in connection with appropriate 
professional and local civic and community organizations and for expenses incurred in 
attendance at appropriate educational and profess ional meetings at the local, state and 
national level.  Assistant Superintendent shall submit p roper vouchers together with 
his/her request for reimbursement in accordance with District policy. 
 6. Termination in Event of Death  - This contract shall be aut omatically 
terminated in the event of the death of Assistant Superintendent. 
 7. Termination for Cause  - This contract may be terminated by District for 
willful violation by Assistant Superintendent of any rules, regulations, directives or 
policies of the Board of Education of the District, violation of any of the terms of this 
contract, or for legal and just cause, including, but not limited to, misconduct or lack of 
legal qualifications. 
 8. Salary Rate on Termination Resulting From Death or Cause  - In the event 
of termination of this contr act as set forth in paragraphs 6 or 7  above, Assistant 
181
Superintendent shall be entitled to salary and benefits only for services rendered to the 
date of termination at the salary rate then in effect. 
 9. Assistant Superi ntendent's Duties  - Assistant Superintendent agrees that 
during the term of this contract he will perform competently and diligently the duties of 
the office of Assistant Superintendent as prescribed by the Board of Education and the 
laws of t he State of M ichigan, and that he will faithfully observe and implement the 
policies, rules, regulations and directives properly adopted and issued by said Board for 
the government of the District. 
 10. Attendance at Board Meetings  - Assistant Superintendent, if physic ally 
able, may be directed to attend any regular meetings and special meetings of the Board of 
Education. 
 11. Indemnification of Assistant Superintendent  - The Board agrees to 
indemnify and hold Assistant Superintendent harmless from and against all claim s, suits 
or administrative proceedings, judgments, damages, liabilities, (including attorney fees, 
costs and expenses) asserted by any third party against Assistant Superintendent arising 
from actions taken or decisions made within the scope of his employment during the term 
of this contract notwithstanding that said claims may be asserted after the termination of 
this Contract; provided, however, that the indemnity given hereby shall not extend to any 
claims, demands, suits or administrative proceedings arising out of the alleged intentional 
tortious actions or inactions (as opposed, for example, to alleged acts of negligence) of 
Assistant Superintendent.  Assistant Superintendent shall give the Board notice of the 
nature of any claim of indemnification her eunder promptly upon receipt of notice of any 
claim or action.  The Board shall have the right to conduct the defense of any such claim 
and Assistant Superintendent shall fully cooperate with the Board in the defense.  
182
Assistant Superintendent may, at Assi stant Superintendent's own cost and expense, 
employ counsel to assist in such defense. 
 12. Exclusion of Tenure in Administrative Capacity  and Non -Renewal - 
Assistant Superintendent shall not be deemed to be granted continuing tenure in the 
capacity of Assistant Superintendent or in any other administrative capacity by virtue of 
this contract of employment or otherwise.  The decision whether to renew or not to renew 
the Agreement with or without cause is solely within the discretion of the Board. 
However, in the event that the Board decides not to renew this Agreement, prior written 
notice of such non -renewal shall be given to the Assistant Superintendent at least ninety 
days (90-days) prior to its expiration date. 
 13. Entire Agreement  - This contract conta ins the entire agreement between 
the parties, and there are no other representations, promises or agreements between them 
whether oral, written, or inferred.  Without limiting the generality of the foregoing, it is 
specifically understood and agreed that t he Administrative Handbook of the Troy School 
District is not applicable to Assistant Superintendent and that no part of said Handbook is 
any of this contract between Assistant Superintendent and the District. 
 14. Applicable Law - This contract is subject  in all respects to all of the laws 
of the state of Michigan, and shall be governed by and interpreted in accordance with the 
laws of the state of Michigan. 
 15.  Arbitration- The Board and the Assistant Superintendent agree to arbitrate 
any and all disput es arising out of this contract under the Employment Rules of the 
American Arbitration Association.  Any arbitration shall be conducted in Oakland 
County, Michigan.  The parties shall equally share the cost of the arbitrator’s fees and 
any hearing room rental fees.  Any award or decision arising out of such arbitration shall 
be enforceable in a court of competent jurisdiction in the state of Michigan.  In the event 
183
of an arbitration arising out of a termination of this Agreement by the Board, the 
arbitrator’s opinion shall be limited to determining whether the Board had just cause 
under the terms of this contract for terminating the  Assistant Superintendent’s 
employment.

Administrative Procedures, if a ny.  Assistant Superintendent acknowledges that 
the district retains the right to amend any performance -based pay plan, which 
amendment, if any, shall apply to Assistant Superintendent.  
198
(8)  IRS SECTION 125 PLAN.  If a Section 125 account is established f or 
administrators covered by the Administrative Handbook, Assistant Superintendent 
shall receive same. 
(9)  MINIMUM BOARD OF EDUCATION FUND BALANCE .  It is expressly 
agreed upon that in the event the Board of Education’s year end audited fund 
balance drops below 12% in any given year, the Board of Education reserves the 
right to make unilateral reductions in total compensation effective the succeeding 
January 1 st of the given year so long as the reduction made to this contract is 
consistent with the percent age reductions required by other bargaining units or 
classes of employee groups within the District.  
    END OF SCHEDULE A

the school shall expend any funds or oblige the expenditure of any funds except pursuant
to appropriations made by the Board of Education in keeping with the budgetary policy
statement hitherto adopted by the Board.  Changes in the amount appropriated by the 
Board shall require approval by the Board.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Superintendent is hereby charged with general 
supervision of the execution of the budget adopted by the Board and shall hold the
department heads responsible for performance of their responsibilities with the amount
appropriated by the Board of Education in keeping with the budgetary policy statement
hitherto adopted by the Board.
This Appropriation Resolution shall take effect immediately.

TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
2016-17 PROPOSED  
GENERAL FUND BUDGET ASSUMPTIONS    
• The projected blended student count of 12,740 is a slight increase from 2015/2016. The 
pupil membership formula changed in 2013/14 to use current fiscal year fall (90%) and 
spring (10%) counts.  Previously, the prior fiscal year spring count was used. 
signature would have a net impact increase our foundation allowance $60 per pupil to 
$9,015.  A majority of this increase will actually be received through a categorical because 
our increase in foundation allowance cannot exceed the rate of inflation.  
• MPSERS Categorical changes are expected to have no net impact on the bottom-line  
budget. 
• 0.75% increase in property tax values. 
Salary & Benefit Items 
• The retirement rate is budgeted with an increase from 36.31% to 36.64% on October 1, 
2016.  The UAAL rate is increasing from 10.53% to 11.70%. The effective retirement rate 
is decreasing 0.84% from 25.78% to 24.94%. The new higher rate will be offset by a 
State funding element called “UAAL Rate Stabilization”.   
• The FICA rate of 7.65% has not changed. 
• Employee benefit costs for health, dental, vision, life and disability are estimated based on 
the State mandated premium caps. The limits for 2016 equal the 2015 limits increased by 
2.5%.  
• We continue work to adjust Salary and Benefits based on information to date.  Overall, 
the assumption is a net increase of 3% in salaries.  The following is a status of the 
employee groups: 
• Teachers (TEA)  
Salaries and benefits reflect the ratified agreement between the TEA and the Board of 
Education. 
• Secretarial (TESA) 
Salaries will include elimination of the wage reduction rates from 15-16; a 6% on-
schedule increase for those employees that will not benefit from step advancement and 2 
full-step improvements for all eligible employees. 
1
231
• The current foundation allowance is $8,955.00 per pupil.  The bill sent to the Governor for 
D R A F T
• Support Personnel (TESPA)
Salaries will include elimination of the 4% off-schedule payment from 2015/16; one full-
step improvement for all eligible employees; and a 5% on-schedule step increase for
employees on the tier 1 schedule who are currently on step 6 or higher.
• Administrators
Administrators salaries have not been decided at the time this document was developed;
however, the budget assumes an increase in salaries for each administrator in alignment
with the agreements reached with our unions.
• Non-Represented
Non-Represented employee salaries have not been decided at the time this document
was developed; however, the budget assumes an increase in salaries for each non-
represented employee in alignment with the agreements reached with our unions.
Other Non-salary Items – 
• Purchased services, supplies and materials, capital outlay, and other were not
significantly changed from the 2015-16 final amendment.

D R A F T
Fiscal Year 2016-17 Grants 
 
 
GENERAL FUND GRANTS 
 
Adult Education Civics ($18,000) – Federal funds to support projects that demonstrate 
effective and innovative practices in providing and increasing access to programs and services 
that integrate English literacy and civics education. 
 
Adult Education Family Literacy ($143,000) – Federal funds to meet the extemporaneous 
needs of the adult education community to upgrade their skills and competencies. 
 
IDEA-Flowthrough ($1,953,352) – Federal funds to provide  instructional services for 
students with learning disabilities, emotional impairments or other eligibility designations as 
outlined in the IEP. 
 
IDEA-Preschool ($58,996) – Federal funds to provide services for “at risk” 3 -6 year-old pre-
primary impaired children and their parents. 
 
Michigan School Readiness ($313,531) – State funded preschool program to serve “at risk” 
four year-old students and their parents. 
 
CTE Millage ($110,944) – County millage  funds to support career and technical education 
programs. 
 
Title I ($600,203) –  Federal funds to help disadvantaged students meet high standards by 
providing instructional services to eligible K-8 students in reading, writing, science, math and 
social studies; provides parental involvement support; and instructional support for homeless 
students who attend the Troy School District. 
 
Title II, Part A, Teacher/Principal Training ($268,756) – Federal funds to implement 
smaller class sizes in grades K-3 and to implement professional development opportunities in 
the district to support building level, district level and curricular student achievement goals. 
 
Title III, English Language Acquisition Program ($306,899) – Federal funds to support 
English language learners from Pre-Kindergarten through 12th grade. 
 
 
 
TROY CAREER CENTER GRANTS 
 
Workforce Investment Act ($2,103,221) – Federal funds  to provide employers and youth 
and adult job seekers with seamless, integrated access to all workforce development 
programs and services operated by the Oakland County Michigan Works! Service Centers.

9. BUSINESS 
C.  CONSIDERATION:  RENEWAL OF FOOD SERVICES CONTRACT 
On June 2, 2015  the Board approved a resolution allowing the 
Administration to enter into a contract with Sodexo Management, Inc. 
to continue to provide the food service operation for the district.  The 
renewal of this contract must be approved by the Michigan Department 
of Education and the Troy Board of Education annually.  The renewal 
of this contract has been approved by the Michigan Department of 
Education. 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below.

     11. A.    06.21.16 
11. OTHER 
A. CONSIDERATION:  Resolution Against Nonpublic School 
               Appropriations  
The Legislature has sent its School Aid Budget proposal to the Governor.   
In Section 152b of the budget, there is an allocation of $2.5  million for 
nonpublic schools.  The appropriation was described by the legislature as 
necessary to cover the costs of state mandates on nonpublic schools that 
were identified in a 2014 MDE report. However, a legal review of the 
language has determined tha t this type of appropriation would likely 
violate the Michigan Constitution which prohibits public resources for 
direct or indirect aid to any private, denominational or other nonpublic 
school. Local school boards and districts  are being asked to pass a 
resolution urging the Governor to veto Section 152b and the $2.5 million 
for nonpublic schools. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the suggested resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: ______________________________ 
Supported By: ______________________________ 
TROY BOARD OF EDUCATION RESOLUTION URGING THE GOVERNOR 
TO VETO APPROPRIATION LEGISLATION FOR NONPUBLIC SCHOOLS 
WITHIN THE STATE OF MICHIGAN 
WHEREAS, the Michigan Legislature has proposed a School Aid Budget which 
allocates $2.5 million for nonpublic schools. 
WHEREAS these funds have been specifically allocated to nonpublic schools as 
“reimbursements” for state-mandated conduct. 
WHEREAS these funds could be utilized to “reimburse” nonpublic schools for 
such things as the costs associated with e mploying staff, including noncertified 
teachers, as well as the provision of mentor teachers, and the expenses 
associated with certain specific areas of instruction, such as government and 
history, among other things. 
269
WHEREAS, Article VIII Sec. 2 of the M ichigan Constitution specifically 
prohibits: 
- the use of public money to aid or maintain a nonpublic school, 
- the use of public money to support the attendance of any student at  
  a public school, and 
- the use of public money to employ an individual at a nonpublic school. 
WHEREAS this appropriation is likely a violation of the Michigan Constitution. 
WHEREAS public schools do not receive similar additional funds specifically 
allocated to reimburse those schools for the costs of these same basic purposes.  
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED  that the Troy Board of Education 
urges the Governor of the State of Michigan to VETO Section 152b of the 
Legislature’s proposed School Aid Budget and thereby uphold the Michigan 
Constitution as well as the will of the people, as demonstrated by multiple prior 
ballot initiatives to fund private schools with public funds, all of which were 
rejected by the citizens of this state.   
Yes: _____________ 
No: _____________ 
PASSED and APPROVED this 21st day of June, 2016 
Troy Board of Education 
Nancy Philippart,    Paula Fleming 
President, Board of Education  Secretary, Board of Education
